<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Worktrees
- Prefer reusing a suitable active worktree. Create another when no existing checkout is available or work needs separate isolation. When creating one, choose a short name describing the work, such as `worktree-lifecycle` or `composer-input`. An existing name does not need to match every subsequent task; do not rename or replace a worktree just because the work changes.
- Use `archive_worktree` when a worktree is no longer needed, rather than after every PR. Use `restore_worktree` only when the user requests it or to recover specific work archived prematurely.
- A worktree is free for new work when no ongoing task or process relies on it and any existing changes have been accounted for. Prepare the appropriate branch and base before starting new work. Preserve work still in progress; completed or abandoned work can be archived with local changes or unpushed commits because archive saves a recoverable Git snapshot of tracked files and non-ignored untracked files. Ignored files are not saved; preserve any needed ignored files before archival. Do not delete files just to make a worktree eligible for archival. Preserve pinned, shared, or in-use worktrees when cleaning up. Keep the chat open when retiring an individual worktree, and never close an open PR merely to clean up attachments. Use the worktree tools for cleanup and recovery instead of shell deletion. Check at these lifecycle transitions, not every turn.
- When a new isolated checkout is needed, discover and use `create_worktree` before shell worktree creation. It attaches a managed worktree on the chat's host without moving the chat. Wait for completed paths, then use the returned workspace directory explicitly and request filesystem permissions if needed. Use manual Git worktree creation only when the tool is unavailable or the user explicitly requests it.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

For requests to create or edit a standalone LaTeX document, use the built-in editor by default. Create or edit the .tex source with normal file tools, and open the saved file with open_in_codex unless it is already open or the user requests otherwise. Keep follow-up edits in that same file and editor. Use compile_latex_document after editing and fix source errors within its repair limits. Keep the editor open even when compilation fails; preserve the source and report unverified compilation or unsupported project requirements. Discover these tools if deferred. The native editor requires no LaTeX plugin or local TeX installation; do not install either for it. Ordinary math explanations stay in chat.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 9.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '# AGENTS.md\r\n\r\nThis file is the Codex-facing adapter for this repository. It summarizes the Claude-oriented project guidance from `CLAUDE.md` in a smaller format that is fast for Codex to scan. `CLAUDE.md` remains the fuller source of project knowledge.\r\n\r\n## Fast Locator\r\n\r\n- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`\r\n- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`\r\n- Live vault knowledge: `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/wiki/` (hubs, voice, business), `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/students/`, `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/corpus/`\r\n- Pre-2026-07-16 LSATU product knowledge (archived, read-only): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`\r\n\r\nIf an older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat it as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.\r\n\r\n## Project Map\r\n\r\n- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.\r\n- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.\r\n- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.\r\n- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.\r\n- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.\r\n- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.\r\n- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.\r\n- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.\r\n\r\n## Stack And Commands\r\n\r\n- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.\r\n- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.\r\n- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.\r\n- Build: `npm run build`\r\n- Lint: `npm run lint`\r\n- Test: `npm run test`\r\n- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`\r\n- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.\r\n- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\r\n\r\n## Working Rules\r\n\r\n- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.\r\n- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.\r\n- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.\r\n- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.\r\n- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.\r\n- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.\r\n- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.\r\n- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.\r\n\r\n## Product Constraints\r\n\r\n- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.\r\n- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.\r\n- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.\r\n- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.\r\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.\r\n\r\n## Smart Drill Note\r\n\r\nOn `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.\r\n\r\n## Verification (must not skip)\r\n\r\n- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not "done" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.\r\n- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed "fix" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.\r\n\r\n## Workflow Expectations\r\n\r\n- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.\r\n- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.\r\n- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.\r\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.\r\n- **After every successful push, publish through Lovable\'s manual Publish control.** Do not prompt the Lovable agent, use a credit-spending action, or change anything else in Lovable. Verify that the manual publish completed before reporting the change as live.\r\n- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.\r\n\r\n## Session Protocol (second brain)\r\n\r\nProduct context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Two repo-local skills in `.agents/skills/` run the session ritual; the canonical protocol is in the vault `CLAUDE.md`.\r\n\r\nThe vault was rebuilt 2026-07-16 (paths relative to `~/ai-os`). Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student\'s record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.\r\n\r\n- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.\r\n- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong "Done" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.\r\n\r\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.\r\n\r\n## Codex Adapter Layout\r\n\r\n- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.\r\n- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. Use `.toml` files with converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\r\n- `.agents/skills/`: repository-local Codex skills copied from `.claude/skills/` when Claude skills exist and Codex should use them. Keep each `SKILL.md` with its supporting files.\r\n- `.codex/hooks.json`: existing repository hooks. Preserve unless the task explicitly concerns hooks.\r\n\r\nThis checkout currently has Claude settings and hooks under `.claude/`, but no `.claude/skills/` or `.claude/agents/` directory to convert.\r\n\r\n## Known Gaps\r\n\r\n- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.\r\n- No Prettier config and no CI workflows.\r\n- `README.md` is still minimal.\r\n\r\n## Agent skills\r\n\r\n### Issue tracker\r\n\r\nGitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\r\n\r\n### Triage labels\r\n\r\nThe five canonical roles, unchanged: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\r\n\r\n### Domain docs\r\n\r\nMulti-context: `CONTEXT-MAP.md` points at eight per-context glossaries; ADRs are all at the root. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '"use client";\r\n\r\nimport * as React from "react";\r\nimport { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";\r\nimport QuestionFinder from "./QuestionFinder";\r\nimport type { QuestionFinderBank } from "./questionFinderSearch";\r\nimport {\r\n  useFinderAddressIndex,\r\n  type FinderAddressSection,\r\n} from "./useFinderAddressIndex";\r\nimport "./questionFinder.css";\r\nimport "./questionJump.css";\r\n\r\nexport interface QuestionJumpProps {\r\n  /** Where a finished one-question attempt returns to. */\r\n  returnTo: string;\r\n  /** The host\'s surface, passed straight through to the search panel. */\r\n  tone?: "light" | "dark";\r\n  /** Active side of the hub switch. */\r\n  bank?: QuestionFinderBank;\r\n  /**\r\n   * Whether the text-search trigger rides at the end of the address row.\r\n   *\r\n   * The drill hub hosts it in the tools row instead, beside the LR/RC switch,\r\n   * so the two utility controls sit on one line rather than stacked. Everywhere\r\n   * else the trigger stays where it was born: at the end of this row.\r\n   */\r\n  withSearch?: boolean;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * The Question jump: PrepTest, section, question — three dropdowns, always open.\r\n *\r\n * The same three selectors used to be one panel deep, behind the finder\'s\r\n * trigger, which made the most ordinary request a student has ("open PT 90,\r\n * section 1, question 12") cost a click, a search field, and a decision about\r\n * what to type. LSAT Lab and 7Sage both put these three in the open, and so\r\n * does this: pick a test, pick a section, pick a question, and you are in it.\r\n *\r\n * It is a sibling of the finder, not a replacement. Addresses are what this\r\n * row does; searching question *text* is still the panel\'s job, and its trigger\r\n * sits at the end of the row.\r\n *\r\n * The third select always lists official question numbers. LR opens the chosen\r\n * question in the drill runner; RC opens its passage at the chosen question.\r\n */\r\nexport default function QuestionJump({\r\n  returnTo,\r\n  tone = "light",\r\n  bank,\r\n  withSearch = true,\r\n}: QuestionJumpProps) {\r\n  const navigate = useNavigate();\r\n  const { tests, loading, failed } = useFinderAddressIndex();\r\n\r\n  const [pt, setPt] = React.useState("");\r\n  const [section, setSection] = React.useState("");\r\n\r\n  const availableTests = React.useMemo(\r\n    () =>\r\n      bank\r\n        ? tests.filter((test) =>\r\n            test.sections.some((entry) =>\r\n              bank === "rc" ? entry.rc.length > 0 : entry.lr.length > 0,\r\n            ),\r\n          )\r\n        : tests,\r\n    [bank, tests],\r\n  );\r\n\r\n  const sections = React.useMemo<FinderAddressSection[]>(() => {\r\n    if (!pt) return [];\r\n    const all = availableTests.find((test) => test.pt === Number(pt))?.sections ?? [];\r\n    if (!bank) return all;\r\n    return all.filter((entry) => (bank === "rc" ? entry.rc.length > 0 : entry.lr.length > 0));\r\n  }, [availableTests, bank, pt]);\r\n\r\n  const activeSection = React.useMemo(\r\n    () => (section ? sections.find((s) => s.section === Number(section)) ?? null : null),\r\n    [section, sections],\r\n  );\r\n\r\n  const isRc = !!activeSection && activeSection.lr.length === 0 && activeSection.rc.length > 0;\r\n\r\n  const rcQuestions = React.useMemo(\r\n    () =>\r\n      (activeSection?.rc ?? []).flatMap((passage) =>\r\n        (passage.questions ?? []).map((question) => ({ ...question, passage })),\r\n      ),\r\n    [activeSection],\r\n  );\r\n\r\n  const thirdLabel = "Question";\r\n\r\n  const openLr = (qnum: string) => {\r\n    const params = new URLSearchParams({\r\n      mode: "type-drill",\r\n      qids: `PT${pt}-S${section}-Q${qnum}`,\r\n      present: "smart",\r\n      returnTo,\r\n    });\r\n    navigate(`/drill?${params.toString()}`);\r\n  };\r\n\r\n  const openRc = (target: string) => {\r\n    const [passageId, questionId] = target.split(":");\r\n    const params = new URLSearchParams({ passage: passageId });\r\n    if (questionId) params.set("q", questionId);\r\n    navigate(`/rc/drill?${params.toString()}`);\r\n  };\r\n\r\n  const thirdDisabled = loading || !activeSection;\r\n  const thirdPlaceholder = !pt\r\n    ? "Pick a test"\r\n    : !section\r\n      ? "Pick a section"\r\n      : isRc\r\n        ? "Question"\r\n        : "Question";\r\n\r\n  return (\r\n    <div className="qj" data-tone={tone}>\r\n      <div className="qj-row" data-search={withSearch ? "true" : "false"} role="group" aria-label="Jump to a question by PrepTest, section, and number">\r\n        <label className="qf-field qj-field">\r\n          <span className="qf-field-label">Test</span>\r\n          <select\r\n            className="qf-select"\r\n            value={pt}\r\n            disabled={loading || failed}\r\n            onChange={(event) => {\r\n              setPt(event.target.value);\r\n              setSection("");\r\n            }}\r\n          >\r\n            <option value="">\r\n              {failed ? "Unavailable" : loading ? "Loading…" : "PrepTest"}\r\n            </option>\r\n            {availableTests.map((test) => (\r\n              <option key={test.pt} value={test.pt}>\r\n                PT {test.pt}\r\n              </option>\r\n            ))}\r\n          </select>\r\n        </label>\r\n\r\n        <label className="qf-field qj-field">\r\n          <span className="qf-field-label">Section</span>\r\n          <select\r\n            className="qf-select"\r\n            value={section}\r\n            disabled={loading || !pt}\r\n            onChange={(event) => setSection(event.target.value)}\r\n          >\r\n            <option value="">{pt ? "Section" : "Pick a test"}</option>\r\n            {sections.map((entry) => (\r\n              <option key={entry.section} value={entry.section}>\r\n                Section {entry.section}\r\n                {entry.lr.length === 0 && entry.rc.length > 0 ? " · RC" : " · LR"}\r\n              </option>\r\n            ))}\r\n          </select>\r\n        </label>\r\n\r\n        {/* Value stays "" on purpose: this select is an action, not a stored\r\n            choice. Picking navigates away, and if the student comes back the\r\n            row should read as untouched rather than claim they are still\r\n            sitting on question 12. */}\r\n        <label className="qf-field qj-field">\r\n          <span className="qf-field-label">{thirdLabel}</span>\r\n          <select\r\n            className="qf-select"\r\n            value=""\r\n            disabled={thirdDisabled}\r\n            onChange={(event) => {\r\n              if (!event.target.value) return;\r\n              if (isRc) openRc(event.target.value);\r\n              else openLr(event.target.value);\r\n            }}\r\n          >\r\n            <option value="">{thirdPlaceholder}</option>\r\n            {isRc\r\n              ? rcQuestions.map((question) => (\r\n                  <option key={question.id} value={`${question.passage.id}:${question.id}`}>\r\n                    Question {question.number} · Passage {question.passage.position}\r\n                  </option>\r\n                ))\r\n              : (activeSection?.lr ?? []).map((qnum) => (\r\n                  <option key={qnum} value={qnum}>\r\n                    Question {qnum}\r\n                  </option>\r\n                ))}\r\n          </select>\r\n        </label>\r\n\r\n        {withSearch ? (\r\n          <div className="qj-search">\r\n            <QuestionFinder returnTo={returnTo} tone={tone} bank={bank} />\r\n          </div>\r\n        ) : null}\r\n      </div>\r\n    </div>\r\n  );\r\n}\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-section-toggle-option {\r\n  background: none;\r\n  border: 0;\r\n  border-radius: 999px;\r\n  color: var(--observatory-stage-muted);\r\n  cursor: pointer;\r\n  font-family: var(--observatory-label-font);\r\n  font-size: var(--observatory-text-label);\r\n  font-weight: 600;\r\n  letter-spacing: var(--observatory-track-route);\r\n  line-height: 1;\r\n  /* 44px by height: the product\'s touch floor, met by the control itself. */\r\n  min-height: 44px;\r\n  padding: 0 16px;\r\n  position: relative;\r\n  transition: color 200ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 0.68, 0.24, 1);\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-section-toggle-option:hover {\r\n  color: var(--observatory-stage-ink);\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-section-toggle-option.is-active {\r\n  color: var(--observatory-stage-lit);\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-section-toggle-option:focus-visible {\r\n  outline: 2px solid var(--observatory-stage-ink);\r\n  outline-offset: 2px;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* The travelling lit slot. Sits behind the label — the label carries\r\n   `position: relative` and a z-index of its own so it is never covered. */\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-section-toggle-indicator {\r\n  background: var(--observatory-stage-ink);\r\n  border-radius: 999px;\r\n  inset: 4px 0;\r\n  position: absolute;\r\n  z-index: 0;\r\n}\r\n\r\n@media (max-width: 719px) {\r\n  /* Narrow: the switch drops under QuestionJump at full width rather than\r\n     shrinking into two unreadable slots. It is never hidden — unlike the route\r\n     label\'s trailing span, which is decoration. */\r\n  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-section-toggle {\r\n    justify-content: center;\r\n    margin-left: 0;\r\n    width: 100%;\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  /* Except when it is riding in the aside beside the search trigger, where\r\n     100% is 100% of a box that already holds the trigger — the switch grew\r\n     past its own container and off the right edge of the screen. In the\r\n     cluster it takes its natural width; the trigger has collapsed to its icon\r\n     by here, so the pair fits on one line. */\r\n  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-tools-aside .drill-observatory-section-toggle {\r\n    width: auto;\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  .drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-section-toggle-option {\r\n    flex: 1 1 0;\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-hero-grid {\r\n  align-items: stretch;\r\n  display: grid;\r\n  gap: 28px;\r\n  grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr) 392px;\r\n  min-height: 400px;\r\n  padding: var(--observatory-space-2) var(--observatory-space-7) var(--observatory-space-7);\r\n  position: relative;\r\n  z-index: 2;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-greeting {\r\n  align-self: end;\r\n  gap: var(--observatory-space-5);\r\n  max-width: 440px;\r\n  min-height: 0;\r\n  padding: 0 0 6px;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-greeting h1 {\r\n  color: var(--observatory-ink);\r\n  font-family: var(--observatory-display-font);\r\n  font-size: clamp(1.9rem, 3.4vw, var(--observatory-text-display));\r\n  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 60;\r\n  font-weight: 400;\r\n  letter-spacing: var(--observatory-track-display);\r\n  line-height: 1.08;\r\n  /* The scrim under the hero is what makes this text readable. A 24px shadow\r\n     at 60% was doing the scrim\'s job a second time, and on a serif at this\r\n     size it reads as a smudge rather than as contrast. */\r\n  text-shadow: 0 1px 2px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.35);\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-greeting[data-next="true"] h1 {\r\n  font-size: clamp(1.5rem, 2.6vw, 2.1rem);\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-greeting h1 em {\r\n  font-style: italic;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-greeting p {\r\n  text-shadow: 0 1px 2px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.3);\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-cta {\r\n  background: var(--observatory-ink);\r\n  border: 0;\r\n  border-radius: 999px;\r\n  box-shadow: 0 6px 20px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.3);\r\n  color: #0c0c0e;\r\n  font-family: "Outfit", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;\r\n  font-size: 14px;\r\n  font-weight: 600;\r\n  gap: 9px;\r\n  height: 46px;\r\n  padding: 0 26px;\r\n  /* background-color is in this list because :hover changes it. It used to be\r\n     animated only by accident, through the Button utility that outranked this\r\n     rule; now that the rule wins on specificity the list has to be honest or\r\n     the hover would snap. */\r\n  transition: background-color var(--observatory-motion), filter var(--observatory-motion),\r\n    transform var(--observatory-motion);\r\n}\r\n\r\n@keyframes drill-observatory-smart-drill-pulse {\r\n  0%,\r\n  100% {\r\n    outline: 0 solid rgba(244, 241, 234, 0);\r\n  }\r\n  50% {\r\n    outline: 5px solid rgba(244, 241, 234, 0.16);\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-smart-drill-pulse {\r\n  animation: drill-observatory-smart-drill-pulse 2.4s ease-in-out infinite;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* No transform on the primary CTA — it is the one fixed point on the page. */\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-cta:hover {\r\n  background: var(--observatory-ink);\r\n  filter: brightness(1.06);\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-cta:active {\r\n  transform: translateY(0.5px);\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* The primary CTA had no focus indicator at all: border 0 plus a shadow, and\r\n   the shared Button ring never reached it through the overrides. One rule for\r\n   every pill-shaped control on the cards. */\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-cta:focus-visible,\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-console-start:focus-visible,\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-secondary-link:focus-visible,\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-insight-action:focus-visible,\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-quiet-link:focus-visible,\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-rc-chip:focus-visible,\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-practice-tile:focus-visible {\r\n  box-shadow: none;\r\n  outline: 2px solid var(--observatory-focus);\r\n  outline-offset: 3px;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-focus-inset {\r\n  outline: none;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-focus-inset:focus-visible {\r\n  box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 2px var(--observatory-focus);\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-skeleton-bar {\r\n  background: var(--observatory-skeleton);\r\n  border-radius: 999px;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* ── Next up, in the hero ─────────────────────────────────────────────── */\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-next {\r\n  display: flex;\r\n  flex-direction: column;\r\n  gap: 6px;\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '/* ── the question jump row ────────────────────────────────────────────────\r\n *\r\n * One band, four controls: three address selects and the text-search trigger.\r\n * It inherits the finder\'s tokens (questionFinder.css defines them on `.qf`,\r\n * and `.qj` repeats the same set) so the two never drift apart visually — they\r\n * are one control surface split across an inline row and a panel.\r\n *\r\n * Rank: this sits under the masthead and above the hero, and must read as a\r\n * utility strip rather than a headline. Hence the sunk surface, the hairline\r\n * rather than a border, and no shadow of its own — it recedes, the hero stays\r\n * the loudest thing on the page. */\r\n\r\n.qj {\r\n  --qj-ink: #332f2c;\r\n  --qj-muted: #6b6662;\r\n  --qj-sunk: #fbf8f3;\r\n  --qj-hair: rgba(68, 64, 61, 0.09);\r\n  --qj-ui: "Outfit", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;\r\n\r\n  /* The field rules (.qf-field-label, .qf-select) are the finder\'s and read\r\n     --qf-* tokens, which only exist under .qf. Outside it they resolved to\r\n     nothing and the row inherited its host\'s ink: ivory on the drill hub\'s\r\n     cream stage, at 1.06:1. The jump row declares its own values under the\r\n     names the rules actually use. */\r\n  --qf-ink: var(--qj-ink);\r\n  --qf-muted: var(--qj-muted);\r\n  --qf-surface: #ffffff;\r\n  --qf-hair-08: var(--qj-hair);\r\n  --qf-accent: #ff8906;\r\n  --qf-ui: var(--qj-ui);\r\n  --qf-mono: "JetBrains Mono", ui-monospace, monospace;\r\n\r\n  width: 100%;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.qj[data-tone="dark"] {\r\n  --qj-ink: #f5f1ec;\r\n  --qj-muted: #8b837b;\r\n  --qj-sunk: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.03);\r\n  --qj-hair: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.1);\r\n  --qf-surface: #17161c;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.qj-row {\r\n  align-items: end;\r\n  background: var(--qj-sunk);\r\n  border-radius: 14px;\r\n  box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px var(--qj-hair);\r\n  display: grid;\r\n  /* The three addresses share equally; search takes only what its trigger\r\n     needs. A fourth equal column made the search button stretch into a slab\r\n     the width of a dropdown, which read as a fourth step in the sequence. */\r\n  grid-template-columns: repeat(3, minmax(0, 1fr)) auto;\r\n  gap: 10px;\r\n  padding: 12px 14px;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* Without the search trigger the row is three addresses and nothing else, so\r\n   the reserved `auto` column would leave a dead gutter on the right. */\r\n.qj-row[data-search="false"] {\r\n  grid-template-columns: repeat(3, minmax(0, 1fr));\r\n}\r\n\r\n.qj-field {\r\n  min-width: 0;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* The trigger is a control, not a field, so it aligns to the selects\' baseline\r\n   rather than to the label row above them. */\r\n.qj-search {\r\n  display: flex;\r\n  align-items: center;\r\n  padding-bottom: 1px;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* Phones: the addresses stay a row of three — breaking them into a column\r\n   turns a three-second jump into a scroll — and search drops beneath. */\r\n@media (max-width: 600px) {\r\n  .qj-row {\r\n    grid-template-columns: repeat(3, minmax(0, 1fr));\r\n    row-gap: 12px;\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  .qj-search {\r\n    grid-column: 1 / -1;\r\n    padding-bottom: 0;\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  .qj-search .qf,\r\n  .qj-search .qf-trigger {\r\n    width: 100%;\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* Touch floor. The selects sat at 39px; 44px is the product\'s binding minimum\r\n   and it is met by height, not by wrapped text. */\r\n.qj .qf-select {\r\n  min-height: 44px;\r\n}\r\n707:.qf-select {\r\n732:.qf-select:hover:not(:disabled) {\r\n736:.qf-select:disabled {\r\n741:.qf-select:focus-visible {\r\n859:/* .qj is listed alongside .qf because the jump row uses .qf-select and now\r\n'}]

13px center, right 8px center;\r\n  background-repeat: no-repeat;\r\n  background-size: 5px 5px, 5px 5px;\r\n  border: 0;\r\n  border-radius: 9px;\r\n  box-shadow: 0 0 0 1px var(--qf-hair-08);\r\n cubic-bezier(0.25, 1, 0.5, 1);\r\n  width: 100%;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* These three are the first controls on the drill hub and had no hover state\r\n   at all: :disabled and :focus-visible existed, nothing not-allowed;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.qf-select:focus-visible {\r\n  outline: 2px solid var(--qf-accent);\r\n  outline-offset: 1px;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* ── keyboard legend flex: none;\r\n  font-size: 11px;\r\n  gap: 16px;\r\n  padding: 10px 20px;\r\n            exact questions before you begin.\r\n          </p>\r\n        </div>\r\n        <Button variant="ghost" onClick={onCancel}>Cancel</Button>\r\n      </div>\r\n\r\n      {/* Container query, not `lg:` — this picker no longer owns a full page.\r\n          It renders inside the drill hub\'s deck, so the viewport can be wide\r\n          while the panel is not. The previous value was 1140px, but\r\n          this container is capped by the classroom shell at ~1084px, so the\r\n          query never matched at any viewport size. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '      /* no-op: see rememberSection in src/lib/drillSection.ts */\r\n    }\r\n  }, [assignmentLeads]);\r\n\r\n  return show;\r\n}\r\n\r\nfunction MainhubHero({\r\n  section,\r\n  firstName,\r\n  drillReturnTo,\r\n  focus,\r\n  onOpenBuilder,\r\n  onStartSmartRc,\r\n  smartDrillPulseActive,\r\n  onSmartDrillClick,\r\n}: {\r\n  section: DrillSection;\r\n  firstName: string;\r\n  drillReturnTo: string;\r\n  focus: TodayFocus;\r\n  onOpenBuilder: () => void;\r\n  onStartSmartRc: () => void;\r\n  smartDrillPulseActive: boolean;\r\n  onSmartDrillClick: () => void;\r\n}) {\r\n  const navigate = useNavigate();\r\n\r\n  /* Same shape as Classroom Home\'s dateline ("TUESDAY · SEPTEMBER 8 · 2026"),\r\n     so the two mounts one click apart do not print the date two ways. */\r\n  const todayLabel = React.useMemo(() => {\r\n    const d = new Date();\r\n    const weekday = d.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { weekday: "long" });\r\n    const monthDay = d.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { month: "long", day: "numeric" });\r\n    return `${weekday} · ${monthDay} · ${d.getFullYear()}`;\r\n  }, []);\r\n\r\n  /* Between 900 and 1099 the greeting column is about 356px, which wraps the\r\n     long date onto a second line. The short month keeps it on one. Both are\r\n     rendered and CSS picks; swapping the string at a JS breakpoint would need\r\n     a resize listener and would not match on the first paint. */\r\n  const todayLabelShort = React.useMemo(() => {\r\n    const d = new Date();\r\n    const weekday = d.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { weekday: "long" });\r\n    const monthDay = d.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { month: "short", day: "numeric" });\r\n    return `${weekday} · ${monthDay} · ${d.getFullYear()}`;\r\n  }, []);\r\n\r\n  const startSmartDrill = React.useCallback(() => {\r\n    navigate("/drill", { state: { mode: "adaptive", returnTo: drillReturnTo } });\r\n  }, [drillReturnTo, navigate]);\r\n\r\n  const startFallback = section === "rc" ? onStartSmartRc : startSmartDrill;\r\n  /* Say what the button does, not what the feature is called. "Smart Drill" is\r\n     the product\'s name for the adaptive set; a student meeting it on a button\r\n     has to guess. The sentence above the button still names it. */\r\n  const fallbackLabel = section === "rc" ? "Read one passage" : "Practice your weakest types";\r\n\r\n  /* The hero leads with the computed next step when there is one. Assigned\r\n     work is section-agnostic (Joshua assigns it, the student does not choose\r\n     a bank first), so it leads on RC too. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '# Guided Drills & Explanations\r\n\r\nThe practice domain: how LSAT U presents drills, the questions inside them, and the teaching that wraps each one. Covers the drill surface (`src/components/drill/`, `src/pages/Drill.tsx`), the question bank (`src/lib/questionLoader.ts`, `public/data/PT*-LR.json`), and the guided-drill catalog (`src/lib/guidedDrills.ts`, `src/data/guidedDrillHtmlManifest.ts`).\r\n\r\n## Language\r\n\r\n**Question Explanation**:\r\nThe question-level teaching shown *after* a student answers one specific question. In the schema this is the `breakdown` (conclusion, conclusionSimple, evidence, justification, objection, prediction, crucialInsight) plus `answerChoiceExplanations` (per-choice verdict + whyCorrect/whyIncorrect) on an `LRQuestion`. One per question. Currently authored for **0 of 3,964** questions — this is the headline gap (Phase 2).\r\n_Avoid_: explanation (ambiguous), breakdown (that\'s only one half), answer key, rationale.\r\n\r\n**Drill Instruction Card**:\r\nThe drill-level teaching panel shown *before* a student starts a guided drill — teaches the pattern the drill targets ("these six questions all hide the conclusion in the first sentence..."). One per guided drill. Stored as `instruction_card: { left_md, right_md }` on a guided drill. The LSAT Lab equivalent is its "Info" card (captured verbatim in `[[LSAT-LAB-LR-INFO-CARDS]]` in the vault).\r\n_Avoid_: info card (that\'s LSAT Lab\'s name), intro, launch card, explanation.\r\n\r\n**Question Type**:\r\nThe LSAT task a question asks — *Main Conclusion, Flaw, Necessary Assumption, Strengthen, Paradox, Must Be True*, etc. Stored as `questionType` on a question (and `question_types[]` on a guided drill). The bank\'s raw values are messy (110 variants, casing dupes, 67 "Unknown") and need a canonical set (~25 types).\r\n_Avoid_: qtype in prose (fine in code), category (that\'s Patterns/Process), question kind.\r\n\r\n**Question Feature**:\r\nA finer-grained pattern tag layered on top of the type — *First Sentence Conclusion, Explain Curious Fact, Anti-Causal, Famous Flaws, Conditional Diagramming, Missing Link / Idea*. Stored as `questionFeatures[]`. 60 clean values; this vocabulary effectively defines the guided-drill taxonomy.\r\n_Avoid_: tag, pattern (overloaded), reasoning type (that\'s a separate field).\r\n\r\n**Reasoning Type**:\r\nThe underlying logical structure — *Causation, Conditional Logic, Comparisons*. Stored as `reasoningType[]`. Distinct from Question Feature.\r\n_Avoid_: using interchangeably with Question Feature.\r\n\r\n**Drill Signature** (proposed):\r\nThe accurate definition of what a guided drill drills = the set of Question Types + Question Features of the *actual questions in its pool* (its qids), NOT the types/features merely mentioned in its Drill Instruction Card text.\r\n_Avoid_: deriving a drill\'s targets from card prose alone (it over-captures).\r\n\r\n**Guided Drill**:\r\nA curated, fixed practice set of LSAT questions with a Drill Instruction Card and (target state) a deterministic ordered question list. 108 exist (`guidedDrillHtmlManifest.ts`).\r\n_Avoid_: drill (too generic — could mean Smart Drill), set, assignment.\r\n\r\n**Answer Navigator** (the "bubbles"):\r\nThe strip along the bottom of the official testing frame: one small empty **oval**\r\nper question in the queue, the question number printed *below* each oval, the\r\ncurrent question\'s number bold with a thin I-beam tick above it. Rendered by\r\n`OfficialAnswerNavigator`. Joshua calls the ovals "bubbles"; the code calls them\r\novals and the strip an answer navigator. Both names mean this one thing.\r\nOne oval stands for one **question**, never for an answer choice. This matters\r\nbecause "bubble" is overloaded: on paper, the bubble you fill in IS your answer\r\n(A-E). Here it is not. An oval is a place to go, so anything that turns it into a\r\nverdict on your answer is out of scope for it by definition.\r\n_Avoid_: pager (that is the RC per-question control that lives in the bar\'s\r\n`pager` slot), dots, progress bar (it is navigation, not a progress readout),\r\nand using "bubble" for an A-E answer choice.\r\n\r\n**Oval State**:\r\nWhat one oval in the Answer Navigator communicates. Today there are exactly three,\r\nand they are orthogonal: **current** (which question you are on), **answered** (a\r\nqid present in `attemptedQids`, meaning at least one recorded attempt), and\r\n**jumpable** (clicking moves you there, governed by `jumpEnabled`). Notably there\r\nis no correct/incorrect state: a section never reveals, so the strip has never had\r\nto say whether an answer was right.\r\n_Avoid_: "completed" or "done" (answered does not mean finished or correct),\r\n"selected" (that is an answer choice, not an oval).\r\n\r\n**Drill Launch**:\r\nEverything the runner needs to be told in order to start a drill: which kind of\r\ndrill, and the name of the particular run. It is carried in the URL and nowhere\r\nelse, so any drill can be refreshed, bookmarked, or reopened. A Drill Launch\r\nnames a run; it does not contain one. The questions, the answers already given\r\nand the position in the queue live in the Run Record.\r\n_Avoid_: route state, config (that word currently means two different things in\r\nthe code, a filter and a fixed question list), params, drill options.\r\n\r\n**Drill Run Spec**:\r\nA Drill Launch after its row has been read: the mode, the question list or the\r\nfilter that draws one, and the ids saying which stored row this run belongs to.\r\nBuilt by `drillRunSpecFromLaunch` and handed to the runner through context by\r\n`DrillLaunchGate`, which is the only way the runner learns what it is running.\r\nThe Drill Launch is what a link says; the Drill Run Spec is what the runner is\r\ntold. One is written and read from a URL, the other never touches one.\r\n_Avoid_: route state (the shape this replaced, and it read the URL a second\r\ntime), drill config (that word already means a filter or a fixed list), session.\r\n\r\n**Run Record**:\r\nThe durable row, per student per run, holding what a run actually consists of:\r\nthe ordered question list, every attempt, the position, and the clock already\r\nspent. Survives a closed tab, a sign-out, and a different device. It is the\r\nreason a Drill Launch can be short: the launch names the run and the Run Record\r\nsupplies its contents. A named generated run is keyed by that name, such as a\r\nBootcamp day and part or a Guided drill id, so a fresh draw cannot strand the\r\nrecord behind a different question list.\r\n_Avoid_: resume state (that is what it is for, not what it is), snapshot (that is\r\none write to it), session (overloaded: `DrillSession` is the in-memory object for\r\nthe current screen, and an auth session is a third thing).\r\n\r\n**Answer** (the pill):\r\nThe on/off control in the session strip that shows which choice is correct.\r\nOn: the correct choice\'s badge goes green, and if the student already submitted\r\na different choice, that one goes red. Off: both colours leave and the choices\r\nread as untouched. It says nothing about *why*; that is the Explanation pill.\r\nNeither direction locks anything and neither direction touches the logged\r\nattempt. Mirrors LSAT Lab\'s Answer button ("Show correct answer" / hide).\r\n_Avoid_: reveal (the code\'s word; fine in code, but "reveal" sounds one-way and\r\nthis is a toggle), solution, show answer.\r\n\r\n**Explanation** (the pill):\r\n            same place it occupies on Classroom Home — the band directly under\r\n            the top of the surface — so it is one control in one position across\r\n            both mounts. No access check here: the whole route is behind\r\n            has_drill_access.\r\n\r\n            Light tone, not dark: the row sits on the cream STAGE, not on a\r\n            card. Dark tone inked its labels ivory-on-cream at 1.07:1, which is\r\n            invisible. The section switch beside it made the same correction\r\n            earlier; the finder\'s trigger is a dark pill either way. */}\r\n        <div className="drill-observatory-tools">\r\n          <QuestionJump\r\n            returnTo={drillReturnTo}\r\n            tone="light"\r\n            bank={section}\r\n            withSearch={false}\r\n          />\r\n          {/* Search and the section switch are one cluster hard right, on the\r\n              same line. The search trigger used to ride at the end of the\r\n              address row, which parked it directly above the LR/RC switch and\r\n              read as a stack of two unrelated controls. Side by side they read\r\n              as what they are: the two utilities of this band. */}\r\n          <div className="drill-observatory-tools-aside">\r\n            <QuestionFinder returnTo={drillReturnTo} tone="dark" bank={section} />\r\n            <SectionToggle section={section} onChange={changeSection} />\r\n          </div>\r\n        </div>\r\n\r\n        {/* The hero stays mounted across section changes (its content swaps in\r\n            place so the LR/RC switch never shows an empty frame), and leaves\r\n            the page entirely while a stage is open. The launcher carries its\r\n            own title and Cancel; keeping a 400px hero above it put "Set up an\r\n            LR drill" on screen twice, 500px apart, and pushed the builder\'s\r\n            own title below the fold. */}\r\n        {stage === "default" && (\r\n          <MainhubHero\r\n            section={section}\r\n            firstName={firstName}\r\n            drillReturnTo={drillReturnTo}\r\n            focus={focus}\r\n            onOpenBuilder={() => openLauncher("type-drill")}\r\n            onStartSmartRc={startSmartRc}\r\n            smartDrillPulseActive={smartDrillPulseActive}\r\n            onSmartDrillClick={() => setSmartDrillPulseActive(false)}\r\n          />\r\n        )}\r\n\r\n        {stage === "analytics" ? (\r\n          <section\r\n            className="drill-observatory-analytics"\r\n            aria-label="Analytics workspace"\r\n            data-tour="mainhub-analytics"\r\n          >\r\n            <AnalyticsStage\r\n              tab={analyticsTab}\r\n              section={section}\r\n1193:.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-hero .drill-observatory-quiet-link {\r\n1464:.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-quiet-link:focus-visible,\r\n1514:.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-quiet-link {\r\n1534:.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-quiet-link:hover {\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '   Drill launcher panels (Full Section / Build a Set)\r\n\r\n   These moved onto the drill hub when the /practice hub was removed. The\r\n   panels themselves (SectionSelector, QuestionPicker) are written entirely\r\n   against shadcn tokens, and this app\'s token set is light-only — dropping\r\n   them onto the hub\'s dark observatory surface would render near-invisible\r\n   grey-on-black. Rather than fork the shared components, re-declare the\r\n   tokens to true-dark values for this subtree only, so the panels inherit a\r\n   dark palette that matches the hero and practice bay above them.\r\n   ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */\r\n.drill-observatory-launcher-panel {\r\n  --background: 240 6% 7%;\r\n  --foreground: 40 20% 96%;\r\n  --card: 240 6% 10%;\r\n  --card-foreground: 40 20% 96%;\r\n  --popover: 240 6% 9%;\r\n  --popover-foreground: 40 20% 96%;\r\n  --primary: 40 20% 96%;\r\n  --primary-foreground: 240 6% 7%;\r\n  --secondary: 240 5% 16%;\r\n  --secondary-foreground: 40 20% 96%;\r\n  --muted: 240 5% 15%;\r\n  --muted-foreground: 40 8% 68%;\r\n  --accent: 240 5% 18%;\r\n  --accent-foreground: 40 20% 96%;\r\n  --border: 0 0% 100% / 0.12;\r\n  --input: 0 0% 100% / 0.14;\r\n  --ring: 40 20% 80%;\r\nconst testSelect = () => screen.getByRole("combobox", { name: /^test$/i });\r\nconst sectionSelect = () => screen.getByRole("combobox", { name: /^section$/i });\r\n\r\ndescribe("QuestionJump", () => {\r\n  beforeEach(() => navigate.mockReset());\r\n\r\n  it("shows all three addresses without anything being opened first", () => {\r\n    mount();\r\n    expect(testSelect()).toBeInTheDocument();\r\n    expect(sectionSelect()).toBeInTheDocument();\r\n    expect(screen.getByRole("combobox", { name: /^question$/i })).toBeInTheDocument();\r\n  });\r\n\r\n  it("opens one LR question in the drill runner, and returns where it started", async () => {\r\n    mount();\r\n    fireEvent.change(testSelect(), { target: { value: "90" } });\r\n    fireEvent.change(sectionSelect(), { target: { value: "1" } });\r\n    fireEvent.change(screen.getByRole("combobox", { name: /^question$/i }), {\r\n      target: { value: "12" },\r\n    });\r\n\r\n    await waitFor(() => expect(navigate).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1));\r\n    const href = navigate.mock.calls[0][0] as string;\r\n    expect(href).toContain("/drill?");\r\n    expect(href).toContain("qids=PT90-S1-Q12");\r\n    expect(href).toContain("returnTo=%2Fclassroom");\r\n  });\r\n\r\n  it("opens the RC passage at the official question selected", async () => {\r\n    mount("rc");\r\n    fireEvent.change(testSelect(), { target: { value: "90" } });\r\n    fireEvent.change(sectionSelect(), { target: { value: "2" } });\r\n\r\n    const third = screen.getByRole("combobox", { name: /^question$/i });\r\n    expect(screen.getByRole("option", { name: "Question 8 · Passage 2" })).toBeInTheDocument();\r\n\r\n    fireEvent.change(third, { target: { value: "1190:708" } });\r\n    await waitFor(() => expect(navigate).toHaveBeenCalledWith("/rc/drill?passage=1190&q=708"));\r\n  });\r\n\r\n  it("shows only sections belonging to the active bank", () => {\r\n    mount("lr");\r\n    fireEvent.change(testSelect(), { target: { value: "90" } });\r\n    expect(screen.getByRole("option", { name: "Section 1 · LR" })).toBeInTheDocument();\r\n    expect(screen.queryByRole("option", { name: "Section 2 · RC" })).toBeNull();\r\n  });\r\n\r\n  it("clears the section when the test changes, so a stale address cannot be opened", () => {\r\n    mount();\r\n    fireEvent.change(testSelect(), { target: { value: "90" } });\r\n    fireEvent.change(sectionSelect(), { target: { value: "1" } });\r\n    expect((sectionSelect() as HTMLSelectElement).value).toBe("1");\r\n\r\n    fireEvent.change(testSelect(), { target: { value: "" } });\r\n    expect((sectionSelect() as HTMLSelectElement).value).toBe("");\r\n    expect(navigate).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\r\n  });\r\n});\r\n   canvas gap supplies the spacing below it, so this row contributes nothing but\r\n   its own height. */\r\n/* The tools row carries two controls now: QuestionJump on the left, the\r\n   LR/RC section switch hard right. It was `display: block` while it held only\r\n   the one. */\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-tools {\r\n  align-items: center;\r\n  display: flex;\r\n  flex-wrap: wrap;\r\n  gap: 12px;\r\n  justify-content: space-between;\r\n  margin-bottom: -4px;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* The address row takes the space that is left; the search trigger and the\r\n   switch keep their intrinsic width and stay together on one line, wrapping as\r\n   a pair rather than one above the other. */\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-tools > .qj {\r\n  flex: 1 1 320px;\r\n  width: auto;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-tools-aside {\r\n  align-items: center;\r\n  display: flex;\r\n  flex: 0 0 auto;\r\n  gap: 12px;\r\n  margin-left: auto;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-tools-aside .drill-observatory-section-toggle {\r\n  margin-left: 0;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* ── Section switch (LR | RC) ────────────────────────────────────────────── */\r\n\r\n/* Note the surface it sits on: the observatory is dark CARDS on a light cream\r\n   stage, and the tools row is on the stage, not on a card. So the switch is\r\n   inked dark-on-light and takes its values from the Question finder beside it\r\n   (questionJump.css light tone) — the two are one utility strip and must not\r\n   read as two different systems. An ivory-on-ivory switch, which the first\r\n   pass shipped, was invisible here. */\r\n.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-section-toggle {\r\n  align-items: center;\r\n  background: var(--observatory-stage-well);\r\n  border-radius: 999px;\r\n  box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px var(--observatory-stage-hair);\r\n  display: inline-flex;\r\n  flex: 0 0 auto;\r\n  gap: 2px;\r\n'}]

## assistant

The white PrepTest menu is the browser’s native `<select>` popup in [QuestionJump.tsx](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/components/questionfinder/QuestionJump.tsx:125). Its closed control is styled, but the open menu cannot be styled reliably. A custom or Radix Select menu is the appropriate change point; its existing tests will need updating.

The drill setup layout and copy are in [QuestionPicker.tsx](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/components/drill/QuestionPicker.tsx:755). The Smart Drill pulse is controlled in [AcademyMainhub.tsx](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/pages/AcademyMainhub.tsx:416) and uses a faint outline animation in [classroom.css](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/components/classroom/classroom.css:1433).

The checkout has many unrelated changes. I made no edits.
